--- FILE docs/modules/logging/microsoft-logging-verification.md SHA256 5f55494d250bba20927b35ad44870c65c579ee991e0e94b4b8f700d36a8c9efe ---
# Microsoft adapter verification wiring

WP06-S03-T03 adds an explicit adapter slice to the shipping-project matrix. The shipping project, assembly and package remain CIS.Core; the slice includes all three handwritten files in Logging/MicrosoftLogger. The separate parser smoke and independently maintained module scopes remain intact. Full shipping matrix execution, source-bound disposition review and both recorded release lanes remain WP07 work.

Run from the repository root after the usual locked restore:

```powershell
pwsh -NoProfile -File implementations/csharp/eng/verify-microsoft-logging.ps1 -Mode Coverage
pwsh -NoProfile -File implementations/csharp/eng/verify-microsoft-logging.ps1 -Mode Mutation
```

Coverage runs the actual provider unit and integration projects separately through Microsoft.CodeCoverage 18.10.1 with the Core module filter and explicit child-process collection. Raw TRX attachments identify each Cobertura report. The runner writes adapter-coverage.json beside each lane report, with exact source observations and report hashes; missing or unexecuted adapter sources fail the run. Adapter source-line observations are extracted by exact source paths, including generated closures mapped to those files, rather than treating the whole Core report as sufficient adapter evidence. The execution observed 65 unit and 79 integration cases, 139/141 adapter lines in each lane. Branch observations are 91/100 unit and 89/100 integration; they remain separate observations, not an invented merged branch result. Coverage mode does not evaluate policy thresholds. The integration branch observation of 0.89 is below the declared 0.90 minimum; threshold qualification remains WP07. These are task observations, not complete release coverage certification. See [Microsoft collector configuration](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/test/customizing-code-coverage-analysis).

Mutation runs Stryker 4.16.0 on its pinned .NET 8.0.31 tool host, with net10.0 tests on 10.0.12 and Complete mutation level. It copies maintained source into a new ignored directory under the repository before building; it never instruments the shared checkout. This source copy is a disposable verification input, not a new canonical implementation. Copy hashes for all snapshot inputs are retained and rechecked after mutation. Original hashes are retained and rechecked for the three adapter sources only. Each run also records the runner, matrix, configuration and toolchain policy hashes. Failed mutation runs keep their native outputs and still verify/rebuild the unmutated copy. The configured 90% score gate is preserved. The runner rejects mismatched matrix/file sets, missing or empty adapter mutant files and a zero testable denominator, regardless of Stryker exit status. No dashboard or remote reporter is selected. See [Stryker configuration](https://stryker-mutator.io/docs/stryker-net/configuration/).

Only in-process provider unit tests currently test injected mutants. Integration children load their own ordinary Core copy; this invocation does not claim child-process mutant injection or give those tests mutation kill credit. Coverage did measure the children independently. The first complete adapter mutation invocation scored 60%, with 78 Killed, 46 Survived and 6 NoCoverage in the testable denominator 130; 23 selected CompileError and 37 selected Ignored were retained without kill credit. Whole-Core mutate-filter omissions remain outside the adapter numerator/denominator. The pre-review final run scored 55.38%: 72 Killed, 51 Survived and 7 NoCoverage, with the same denominator 130, 23 selected CompileError and 37 selected Ignored. Native per-test coverage selections and results differed between runs; both native observations are retained. The revised runner was then executed in both modes: its mutation run scored 54.62%, with 71 Killed, 51 Survived and 8 NoCoverage out of 130 testable mutants. The report guards passed, the native threshold command exited 2 and the unmutated rebuild passed. These differing native selections/results require repeatability investigation in WP07; their cause is not established here. This exposes remaining mutation/test and native-exclusion review work in WP07; it is not a passing mutation release gate or a reason to lower thresholds.

The module matrix links the actual unit/integration coverage commands and dedicated Stryker config to the same Core shipping identity. The mutation family references that matrix explicitly; fast Developer verification remains separate. No public API, shipping dependency, helper assembly or extra runtime worker is added. Original source plans and full release requirements are preserved. Candidate package compatibility, release bundle and runnable examples/activated instructions remain later tasks.


--- FILE implementations/csharp/eng/verify-microsoft-logging.ps1 SHA256 b98e82d80b7e47ab13636693d170f237cc5b9ca7f9e832e7cbba9c460a56ad34 ---
param(
    [ValidateSet('Coverage', 'Mutation')][string] $Mode = 'Coverage',
    [string] $OutputRoot
)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
$languageRoot = Split-Path $PSScriptRoot -Parent
$repositoryRoot = Split-Path (Split-Path $languageRoot -Parent) -Parent
$policy = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'toolchain.json') | ConvertFrom-Json
if (-not $OutputRoot) { $OutputRoot = Join-Path $repositoryRoot ('.artifacts/csharp/microsoft-logging/' + [guid]::NewGuid().ToString('N')) }
$OutputRoot = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath($OutputRoot)
if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $OutputRoot) { throw 'Use a new output directory; retained reports are immutable.' }
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'logs') -Force | Out-Null
$commands = [Collections.Generic.List[object]]::new()
$matrix = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath (Join-Path $languageRoot 'verification/shipping-projects.json') | ConvertFrom-Json
$slice = @($matrix.shippingProjects.moduleSlices | Where-Object id -eq 'logging-microsoft')
if ($slice.Count -ne 1) { throw 'Expected one Microsoft logging matrix slice.' }
$expectedPaths = @($slice[0].sources | ForEach-Object { 'implementations/csharp/' + $_ } | Sort-Object)
$sourceInputs = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath (Join-Path $languageRoot 'src/CIS.Core/Logging/MicrosoftLogger') -File -Filter '*.cs' | ForEach-Object {
    @{ path = [IO.Path]::GetRelativePath($repositoryRoot, $_.FullName).Replace('\', '/'); sha256 = (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() }
})
if (@(Compare-Object $expectedPaths @($sourceInputs.path | Sort-Object)).Count -ne 0) { throw 'Adapter source files differ from the matrix.' }
$verificationInputs = @($PSCommandPath, (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'toolchain.json'), (Join-Path $languageRoot 'verification/shipping-projects.json'), (Join-Path $languageRoot 'verification/mutation/microsoft-logging-config.json'), (Join-Path $languageRoot 'verification/coverage/microsoft-logging.runsettings')) | ForEach-Object {
    @{ path = [IO.Path]::GetRelativePath($repositoryRoot, $_).Replace('\', '/'); sha256 = (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $_ -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() }
}
$verificationInputs | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5 | Set-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'verification-inputs.json') -Encoding utf8
$sourceInputs | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5 | Set-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'adapter-source-inputs.json') -Encoding utf8
function Invoke-Recorded {
    param([string] $Name, [string[]] $Arguments)
    $log = Join-Path $OutputRoot "logs/$Name.log"
    & dotnet @Arguments 2>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath $log | Out-Host
    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
    $commands.Add(@{ name = $Name; executable = 'dotnet'; argv = $Arguments; workingDirectory = (Get-Location).Path; exitCode = $code; log = "logs/$Name.log" })
    $commands | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 7 | Set-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'commands.json') -Encoding utf8
    if ($code -ne 0) { throw "$Name failed with exit $code. Native outputs retained." }
}
function Write-CoverageObservation {
    param([string] $Directory)
    [xml] $trx = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Directory 'report.trx')
    $attachments = @($trx.SelectNodes('//*[local-name()="UriAttachment"]/*[local-name()="A"]') | Where-Object { $_.GetAttribute('href') -like '*.cobertura.xml' })
    if ($attachments.Count -ne 1) { throw 'Expected one TRX Cobertura attachment per lane.' }
    $attachmentName = $attachments[0].GetAttribute('href').Replace('\', '/').Split('/')[-1]
    $reports = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Directory -Recurse -File | Where-Object Name -eq $attachmentName)
    if ($reports.Count -eq 0) { throw 'Cannot resolve the TRX Cobertura attachment.' }
    $hashes = @($reports | ForEach-Object { (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Algorithm SHA256).Hash } | Sort-Object -Unique)
    if ($hashes.Count -ne 1) { throw 'Conflicting copies of the TRX Cobertura attachment.' }
    $report = $reports[0]
    [xml] $xml = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $report.FullName
    $observations = foreach ($input in $sourceInputs) {
        $fullPath = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath((Join-Path $repositoryRoot $input.path))
        $classes = @($xml.SelectNodes('//class') | Where-Object { $_.GetAttribute('filename').Replace('/', '\') -eq $fullPath.Replace('/', '\') })
        if ($classes.Count -eq 0) { throw "Adapter source absent from coverage: $($input.path)" }
        $lines = @{}
        foreach ($class in $classes) {
            foreach ($line in $class.SelectNodes('./lines/line')) {
                $number = $line.GetAttribute('number')
                $hits = [int]$line.GetAttribute('hits')
                $covered = 0; $total = 0
                if ($line.GetAttribute('branch') -ieq 'true') {
                    if ($line.GetAttribute('condition-coverage') -notmatch '\((\d+)/(\d+)\)') { throw 'Unrecognized native branch observation.' }
                    $covered = [int]$Matches[1]; $total = [int]$Matches[2]
                }
                if ($lines.ContainsKey($number)) {
                    $previous = $lines[$number]
                    if ($previous.total -gt 0 -and $total -gt 0 -and $previous.total -ne $total) { throw 'Conflicting branch denominators for a duplicate source line.' }
                    $total = [Math]::Max($total, $previous.total)
                    $hits = [Math]::Max($hits, $previous.hits); $covered = [Math]::Max($covered, $previous.covered)
                }
                $lines[$number] = @{ hits = $hits; covered = $covered; total = $total }
            }
        }
        $values = @($lines.Values)
        $coveredLines = @($values | Where-Object hits -gt 0).Count
        if ($values.Count -eq 0 -or $coveredLines -eq 0) { throw "Adapter source has no measured execution: $($input.path)" }
        @{ path = $input.path; linesCovered = $coveredLines; linesTotal = $values.Count; branchesCovered = ($values | Measure-Object covered -Sum).Sum; branchesTotal = ($values | Measure-Object total -Sum).Sum }
    }
    @{
        files = @($observations)
        trxSha256 = (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Directory 'report.trx') -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()
        coberturaSha256 = (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $report.FullName -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()
        thresholdEvaluation = 'Deferred to WP07; these are separate lane observations, not release qualification.'
    } | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 7 | Set-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Directory 'adapter-coverage.json') -Encoding utf8
}
function Write-MutationObservation {
    param([string] $ReportPath)
    $report = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $ReportPath | ConvertFrom-Json
    $files = @($report.files.PSObject.Properties | Where-Object { $_.Name.Replace('\', '/') -match '/Logging/MicrosoftLogger/' })
    $relative = @($files | ForEach-Object { 'implementations/csharp/src/CIS.Core/Logging/MicrosoftLogger/' + [IO.Path]::GetFileName($_.Name) } | Sort-Object)
    if (@(Compare-Object $expectedPaths $relative).Count -ne 0) { throw 'Native mutant files differ from the adapter matrix.' }
    $counts = @{}
    foreach ($file in $files) {
        $mutants = @($file.Value.mutants)
        if ($mutants.Count -eq 0) { throw "Adapter source has zero mutants: $($file.Name)" }
        foreach ($mutant in $mutants) {
            if (-not $counts.ContainsKey($mutant.status)) { $counts[$mutant.status] = 0 }
            $counts[$mutant.status]++
        }
    }
    $denominator = 0
    foreach ($status in @('Killed', 'Timeout', 'Survived', 'NoCoverage')) {
        if ($counts.ContainsKey($status)) { $denominator += $counts[$status] }
    }
    if ($denominator -eq 0) { throw 'Adapter mutation denominator is zero.' }
    @{ nativeReportSha256 = (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $ReportPath -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant(); counts = $counts; testableDenominator = $denominator; scope = 'Matrix adapter files only; ignored/noncompiling mutants receive no kill credit.' } |
        ConvertTo-Json -Depth 7 | Set-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'adapter-mutation.json') -Encoding utf8
}

Push-Location $languageRoot
try {
    if ((& dotnet --version).Trim() -ne $policy.sdk) { throw 'Selected SDK differs from toolchain policy.' }
    if ($Mode -eq 'Coverage') {
        foreach ($family in @('Unit', 'Integration')) {
            $project = "tests/CIS.Core.$($family)Tests/CIS.Core.$($family)Tests.csproj"
            Invoke-Recorded "build-$family" @('build', $project, '-c', 'Release', '--no-restore')
            $directory = Join-Path $OutputRoot $family.ToLowerInvariant()
            Invoke-Recorded "coverage-$family" @('test', $project, '-c', 'Release', '--no-build', '--no-restore', '--filter', "FullyQualifiedName~CIS.Tests.$($family)Tests.Provider", '--settings', 'verification/coverage/microsoft-logging.runsettings', '--collect', 'Code Coverage;Format=cobertura', '--results-directory', $directory, '--logger', 'trx;LogFileName=report.trx')
            Write-CoverageObservation $directory
        }
    }
    else {
        # Stryker must never write instrumented sources/assemblies into the shared checkout.
        $snapshot = Join-Path $OutputRoot 'source'
        Push-Location $repositoryRoot
        try {
            $files = @(& git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard -- implementations/csharp)
            if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Cannot enumerate maintained mutation inputs.' }
            $files += 'README.md'
            $inventory = foreach ($relative in ($files | Sort-Object -Unique)) {
                $source = Join-Path $repositoryRoot $relative
                $copy = Join-Path $snapshot $relative
                New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path (Split-Path $copy -Parent) -Force | Out-Null
                Copy-Item -LiteralPath $source -Destination $copy
                @{ path = $relative; sha256 = (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $copy -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() }
            }
            $inventory | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5 | Set-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'snapshot-inputs.json') -Encoding utf8
        }
        finally { Pop-Location }
        $isolatedLanguage = Join-Path $snapshot 'implementations/csharp'
        Set-Location $isolatedLanguage
        Invoke-Recorded 'tool-restore' @('tool', 'restore', '--configfile', 'eng/Tools.NuGet.config')
        Invoke-Recorded 'restore-unit' @('restore', 'tests/CIS.Core.UnitTests/CIS.Core.UnitTests.csproj', '--locked-mode', '--configfile', 'NuGet.config')
        Invoke-Recorded 'build-unit' @('build', 'tests/CIS.Core.UnitTests/CIS.Core.UnitTests.csproj', '-c', 'Release', '--no-restore')
        Invoke-Recorded 'baseline-unit' @('test', 'tests/CIS.Core.UnitTests/CIS.Core.UnitTests.csproj', '-c', 'Release', '--no-build', '--no-restore', '--filter', 'FullyQualifiedName~CIS.Tests.UnitTests.Provider', '--logger', 'trx;LogFileName=baseline.trx', '--results-directory', (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'baseline'))
        $packageDirectory = ((& dotnet nuget locals global-packages --list) -join '').Split(': ', 2)[1].Trim()
        $tool = Join-Path $packageDirectory 'dotnet-stryker/4.16.0/tools/net8.0/any/Stryker.CLI.dll'
        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $tool)) { throw 'Pinned Stryker entry point was not materialized.' }
        Set-Location (Join-Path $isolatedLanguage 'src/CIS.Core')
        $mutationFailure = $null
        try {
            Invoke-Recorded 'mutation' @('exec', '--fx-version', $policy.toolHostRuntime, '--roll-forward', 'Disable', $tool, '--config-file', '../../verification/mutation/microsoft-logging-config.json', '--output', (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'mutation'))
        }
        catch { $mutationFailure = $_ }
        finally {
            try { Write-MutationObservation (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'mutation/reports/mutation-report.json') }
            finally {
                foreach ($input in $inventory) {
                    if ((Get-FileHash -LiteralPath (Join-Path $snapshot $input.path) -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() -ne $input.sha256) { throw "Mutation changed a retained source input: $($input.path)" }
                }
                Set-Location $isolatedLanguage
                Invoke-Recorded 'unmutated-rebuild' @('build', 'tests/CIS.Core.UnitTests/CIS.Core.UnitTests.csproj', '-c', 'Release', '--no-restore', '--no-incremental')
            }
        }
        if ($mutationFailure) { throw $mutationFailure }
    }
    Write-Output "Microsoft logging $Mode completed. Native reports: $OutputRoot"
}
finally {
    try {
        foreach ($input in $sourceInputs) {
            if ((Get-FileHash -LiteralPath (Join-Path $repositoryRoot $input.path) -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() -ne $input.sha256) { throw 'Canonical adapter source changed during verification.' }
        }
    }
    finally { Pop-Location }
}

--- FILE implementations/csharp/verification/mutation/README.md SHA256 dd76e3f93baf4e81986b91dd4f2c21372acdb465c01b6c5143bb031d27ff402b ---
# Mutation verification

Mutation is the sixth family. Stryker is a pinned development CLI restored through the language-local tool manifest and dedicated tool source mapping. Core, its lockfile and its packed dependencies contain no Stryker reference.

Run from the repository root:

```powershell
pwsh -File implementations/csharp/eng/verify-toolchain.ps1
```

The driver copies maintained source into a unique `.artifacts/csharp/toolchain/<run>/source` directory and uses fresh CLI and NuGet caches. Stryker **4.16.0** runs on the explicitly selected .NET **8.0.31** tool host with roll-forward disabled, mutates the genuine Core severity parser with the `Complete` mutation level, and runs its MSTest cases through **VSTest** against `net10.0`. Local JSON/HTML reporters are enabled; no dashboard reporter is selected.

[stryker-config.json](stryker-config.json) is the retained smoke scope, not a future full-release mutation matrix. The parser run remains a smoke scope. Core now contains logging/provider and other module implementations; their full shipping scope and materially different targets must be covered by the WP07 release matrix.

The driver rejects zero valid mutants and checks `Killed / (Killed + Timeout + Survived + NoCoverage)` at 90% or above. Every testable mutant must be killed or explicitly diagnosed; native noncompiling/ignored mutants require a source/tool-bound review in `smoke-dispositions.json`. A changed source hash, tool pin or unexpected exclusion fails qualification. No excluded mutant receives kill credit. Native reports remain immutable. Source hashes must match before/after mutation, followed by a fresh unmutated build, pack and shipping dependency check in the isolated copy.

Read [toolchain qualification](../../../../docs/architecture/csharp-toolchain.md) for reports, prerequisites and retention limits. Retain the complete local run directory; generated caches and packages are ignored by Git.

## Embedded Microsoft adapter

`microsoft-logging-config.json` selects all three `Logging/MicrosoftLogger/*.cs` source files within CIS.Core at Complete mutation level and runs the actual provider unit tests. `eng/verify-microsoft-logging.ps1 -Mode Mutation` executes it in a maintained-source copy beneath the ignored .artifacts folder, with pinned tool/runtime and local JSON/HTML output. Integration child processes are measured separately by coverage; this invocation does not claim to inject mutants into those children. Native non-killed/ignored/noncompiling observations keep their statuses, without kill credit or automatic exclusions. A below90% score stays a failed mutation gate. Full source-bound dispositions, gap fixes and both recorded platform lanes remain WP07 work.

--- FILE planning/logging/plan-amendments.md SHA256 159ee25754bf50e051f0d6cde729da4ed2d00db28f2bbf82d9398aa063c4b29c ---
# Amendments to the supplied logging plans

Effective 10 October 2026. The user's architecture and dependency decisions override the corresponding design choices in the source guide/plan/backlog. Original files and their hashes remain unchanged; this record preserves their provenance without executing conflicting instructions.

| Source area | Current direction |
| --- | --- |
| Repository foundation and language portability | CisLib has a separate repository. C# is first; shared contracts/conformance apply to future languages with declared runtime capabilities. The source TypeScript follow-on is a candidate, not the only possible future implementation. |
| REQ-005; guide section 3; WP01-S02 package-reference/bridge boundaries | Core may explicitly reference approved Microsoft-maintained packages. Verify the full resolved runtime closure and packed dependency groups. Third-party runtime dependencies stay in separate optional integration packages. Zero PackageReference items is superseded. |
| Guide sections 5/10; WP01-S01/WP01-S02/WP06 standalone bridge identities | One Core project/package contains the multi-module implementation. Logging and MicrosoftLogger are folders/namespaces inside Core. Microsoft integration remains required planned functionality; its separate package/project requirement is superseded. |
| WP06/WP07/WP08 separate bridge checks and candidate identities | Preserve provider mapping, filter, scope and ownership observations. Qualify them against the Core candidate and include the provider code in Core's architecture, coverage/mutation and compatibility scope. Independent bridge package restoration and a separate bridge assembly are no longer applicable. |
| Public library naming | Project, assembly and package ID are `CIS.Core`; the root namespace is `CIS`. Module namespaces begin `CIS.Logging`, not `CIS.Core.Logging`. |
| First consuming application | A simple ConsoleHuman example host is the initial consumer. It owns the runtime once implemented. A source project reference is explicit development scaffolding; release examples must consume exact tested packages. |
| CIS integration backlog | CisLib produces versioned library/reference artifacts; CIS owns their import, managed installation and routing. A library release and a CIS release remain separately identified. |

The source behavioral contracts, bounded lifecycle requirements and verification families remain the proposed implementation baseline. No logging story is marked Done by initialization. The public facade spelling is amended below. Indefinite compatibility policy, licence and release policy still need their publication disposition.

## Severity aliases selected during WP01-S03

The user expanded the accepted configuration tokens on 10 October 2026. In addition to the seven full severity names, accept `TRC` (Trace), `DBG` (Debug), `INF`/`INFO` (Information), `WRN`/`WARN` (Warning), `ERR` (Error) and `CRT`/`CRIT` (Critical), case-insensitively after trimming. This supersedes the source guide's full-name-only token list. Blank values remain absent; unrecognized and numeric tokens remain invalid. Serialization status mappings are unchanged. Preserve this parser and its tests for WP03-S02; the public logging API is still pending.

## Startup facade name selected during WP02-S01

The user explicitly selected **`EnvironmentOptions`** before publication, replacing the source guide/plan's **`EnviormentsOptions`** spelling. Use `CIS.EnvironmentOptions` for the proposed C# facade. This supersedes the original naming passages in REQ-002, WP01-S01, WP02-S01, WP03-S04 and subsequent consumer examples; the preserved source files and historical evidence remain unchanged. No misspelled compatibility alias is required because neither name has been published.

WP02-S01 specifies the surface and portable expectations using a nonshipping reference-only assembly and compiled consumer fixtures. Runtime metadata/configuration resolution and static publication remain WP03 implementation work. Selected type/property/code details are implementation choices recorded in [the startup contract](../../docs/modules/logging/startup-contract.md), rather than additional directions attributed to the user.

## Entry-size limit and truncation selected during WP02-S02

The user explicitly requested a maximum entry size with an environment override exposed through EnvironmentOptions, suggesting 64 KiB as the default, and then specified that oversized entries must be truncated rather than rejected. This supersedes guide section 9's fixed 64 KiB cap/ordinary oversize rejection, WP02-S02-AC03's runtime rejection direction and later capture/admission oversize cases. An already serialized record above the selected cap remains invalid output in contract fixtures; the runtime must turn oversized input into a valid bounded record.

Selected before publication: CORE_LOG_MAX_ENTRY_BYTES, LoggingSetup.MaxEntryBytes and LoggingOptions.MaxEntryBytes; default 65536 bytes, valid range 16384ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“16777216. Names/range are maintainer choices, not user-quoted values. Preserve protected identity/correlation and types, clip payload at scalar boundaries, omit excess children/whole explicit numeric strings and emit top-level truncated:true. Count the whole actual encoded entry including escapes and LF; all output formats are bounded. The minimum leaves room for the protected envelope. Malformed data and unsupported types remain invalid; an unrepresentable protected envelope produces a specific error, never an oversized record. Scope/node/depth and text bounds use bounded projection with truncation provenance. Scope misuse remains an error. [Event guidance](../../docs/modules/logging/event-contract.md) specifies deterministic priority and pending runtime evidence.

An enabled file output requires FileMaxBytes >= MaxEntryBytes, so an admitted bounded entry can fit a complete segment; a smaller segment setting reports OutOfRange. This cross-option rule is a prepublication implementation choice prompted by the configurable entry cap. Disabled outputs still validate each selected option individually.

## Runtime observations selected during WP02-S03

The [runtime contract](../../docs/modules/logging/runtime-contract.md) defines logger/runtime/provider signatures, result observations and ownership without adding shipping placeholders. The guide's outcome/status/completion enum numbers, decision order, accepted-only tickets, bounded coordination and shared shutdown deadline remain intact.

Prepublication maintainer choices include result/diagnostic type names, safe error codes, BoundaryCapturedAtUtc, writer-flush/runtime/output-state enums and a nonowning-only CoreLoggerProvider constructor in CIS.Logging.MicrosoftLogger. To expose the user-selected truncation policy, LogWriteResult.IsTruncated and global TruncatedEventCount report valid captures with omitted payload. LossObserved includes that captured count; successful draining may therefore coexist with reported payload loss. These property names and accounting details were selected for implementation, not quoted as further user instructions. Counter fixture transport uses exact decimal strings, without changing event JSON.

WP02-S03 qualification executes pure admission/result specification checks and reference-only compiler consumers. Lifecycle/provider/worker expectations remain pending runtime execution. No disk durability, collector acknowledgement, automatic recovery or cross-output order is promised.

## Scope ownership selected during WP04-S03-T03

On 11 October 2026 the user selected: "Keep BeginScope and the creator-disposes rule; document the detection limits." Keep the original guide section10 and WP04-S03-AC03/AC04 direction: scopes are disposed LIFO by their creator, repeated valid disposal is harmless, invalid order leaves the head unchanged, normal child contexts inherit values, and transferring handles to another concurrent flow is prohibited.

This withdraws only the later unpublished WP02-S03 draft's universal cross-flow exception promise. Native Windows/Ubuntu investigations and read-only Claude review showed that inherited frames, ExecutionContext identity, AsyncLocal tokens and Task.CurrentId cannot prove an arbitrary async-flow owner. There is no published baseline to change; original supplied sources, reference signatures and historical evidence remain preserved.

The current implementation's precise detectable guards reject a non-head ancestor, or an absent frame before the handle has completed any pop, without changing the current head. The completion flag records any pop, including prohibited misuse; it is not ownership evidence. Other prohibited handle misuse has unspecified behavior. Restored stale contexts and unawaited children can retain an old frame after the creator has popped; an async helper cannot install its own scope in its caller by returning a handle. The callback alternative is not selected; Microsoft scope integration still needs its required IDisposable surface. Public runtime/provider integration and complete shared-story qualification remain later tasks.

## User-directed passive environment store

The revised EnvironmentOptions-Codex-Simplification (1).md supersedes the earlier initializer simplification and the unpublished startup discovery/publication mechanics selected in WP03-S01 through WP03-S04. EnvironmentOptions only stores values with public setters and literal defaults. Caller startup supplies initial facts and decides when consumers start. EnvironmentOverrides applies known variables explicitly; LoggingOptions prepares its own settings and EventMetadata owns logging's captured facts. There is no initialization/readiness/locking/retry/snapshot framework, no automatic getter loading and no future general startup pipeline in this task.

The publication ledger is empty. Remove EnvironmentSetup, EnvironmentSnapshot, LoggingSetup and lifecycle members; update current reference consumers without a compatibility bridge. Existing variable meanings, bounds, severity aliases, BuildVersion/service-version distinction and lexical path behavior remain. Error owner keys and exact issue positions are draft implementation choices; current fixtures disclose their migration. Preserve historical source plans and execution receipts. All six release families and pending runtime/provider/package qualifications remain required. This is separately authorized correction work while the broader logging roadmap is paused.

## User-requested explicit default population

The user asked to close the missing heuristics/default-population gap after the passive-store correction. EnvironmentDefaults.Apply(applicationAssembly) now supplies common assembly, host, runtime/process, UUID and UTC facts through one explicit call. The store remains passive. Caller startup applies defaults, then explicit assignments, then known environment overrides before starting consumers. Environment name/kind, build/commit identifiers and logging values remain caller-controlled. Repeated helper calls reapply defaults rather than using lifecycle/readiness/publication state. This follow-up does not resume the paused logging roadmap, publish an API or introduce a general startup pipeline. The current guide and separate environment-defaults implementation record describe exact behavior and executed evidence.

## User-selected readonly static environment construction

The latest direct user request makes obvious runtime/identity facts readonly and populates defaults followed by overrides in the constructor, with common identity strings interned. The user explicitly selected keeping the static container with a static constructor. This supersedes the passive-store attachment and explicit EnvironmentDefaults.Apply candidate where they conflict. First access discovers entry-assembly and current runtime/process facts and applies existing alias/canonical variables. Fifteen properties are getter-only; EnvironmentKind, CommitId and BuildId remain ordinary caller policy, and the getter-only Logging reference contains mutable module-owned options. Four bounded validated identity strings are interned after override selection. No explicit initializer, reset/readiness state, snapshot transport, runtime startup or new dependency is introduced. CLR static failure semantics are documented and exercised. Controlled test transports preserve useful metadata/validation vectors without mutating readonly production facts. The nine migrated mechanic bindings are recorded explicitly; all 41 Planned release cases remain unchanged. The broader logging roadmap remains paused and the changes remain uncommitted.

Fixed safe categories and this ordering are maintainer implementation choices within the task. Invalid arguments remain argument errors, and physical file ownership/public bounded lifecycle/full S04 consumers remain required later work.

## WP05-S04-T04 static-container consumer qualification

The [same compiled console consumer](../../docs/modules/logging/console-consumers.md) reads real environment variables through the selected static EnvironmentOptions constructor, then prepares module settings before internal runtime composition. This replaces the original snapshot wording without reintroducing superseded publication APIs. Literal actual-stream and controlled worker cases plus source-preserving criterion mapping qualify the task; default Human and explicit JSONL CLI selection remain distinct configuration facts. Public runtime/physical file/bounded lifecycle/provider/release remain later work. These are implementation/qualification choices within the authorized roadmap, not new user policy.

## WP05-S05-T01 physical acquisition boundary

The owner consumes the prepared absolute path and acquires physical handles only at initialization. A persistent empty `.cis.lock` companion identifies the configured path group; an actual second-handle probe checks exclusivity before active-file creation. The active file permits readers and is opened without truncation or append. Nonempty companion collisions and unavailable ownership fail clearly with fixed safe codes. Existing artifacts are preserved and acquired handles are released. Group aliases that require different companion paths are outside this ownership protocol. Format inventory and permission/format/full startup qualification remain the following S05 tasks; this foundation does not activate public runtime examples or approve a release.

## WP05-S05-T02 inventory foundation

The format-aware internal owner acquires the path lease before inventory I/O, creates metadata only for a new empty group, and otherwise validates immutable group/segment identity and group-wide format/UTF-8/no-BOM/LF facts without rewriting existing bytes. Internal metadata bounds are 2 MiB/depth12/1001entries. Exact `.cis-log-<groupN>-<idD20>.closed|damaged` names fence referenced members to the same directory and provide the later rotation basis. Missing active members fail clearly; startup never deletes pre-existing/nonempty artifacts. No append, worker/public-runtime composition, rotation, recovery or release qualification is claimed.

## WP05-S05-T03 actual startup composition

The [file/console composition](../../docs/modules/logging/file-startup.md) freezes only concrete consumed settings, starts unpublished idle workers, validates borrowed console readiness and acquires format-aware physical file ownership before returning the internal runtime. File resources close outside admission before worker completion; failed startup joins only its own workers and preserves pre-existing artifacts/borrowed streams. Unbuffered managed writes make completion observable without durability claims. Existing oversize/non-LF boundaries fail safely until S06 rotation and S07 selected recovery replace the explicit guards. These are scoped implementation choices, not new public startup abstractions, release approval or a withdrawal of later roadmap requirements.

## WP05-S05-T04 physical runtime qualification

The [startup qualification](../../docs/modules/logging/file-startup-qualification.md) uses real live process-owned configured runtimes, literal producer PID/order checks, captured relative/Unicode paths, immutable format inventory failures and actual OS-denied permissions. Narrow deny rules/modes affect only fresh native temporary paths and are restored in finally; exclusive probes before child exit qualify acquired-handle release; later retry qualifies restored-path reuse. This is verification within the authorized task, without shipping API/dependency changes or a new startup framework. All four S05 criteria are mapped at the internal physical-runtime seam; public lifecycle, rotation/recovery and release requirements remain separate.

## WP05-S06-T01 complete-record size rotation

[Size rotation](../../docs/modules/logging/file-rotation.md) replaces the temporary post-start file capacity fault with actual whole-record rotation under the existing path-group lease. Initial physical length and successful prepared-byte writes supply the count; LF is included. Minimal closed/new-active inventory publication is necessary for this task and uses the existing persistent group and increasing segment IDs. It occurs before fresh-active creation, so failed publication leaves no appendable active path. This is an implementation-order choice within the authorized roadmap, without a new dependency/public API/framework. Configured retention/restart qualification remains T02; exact deletion/failure policy remains T03/T04; interrupted rotation recovery remains S07. Existing startup guards, source plans, historical evidence and all41Planned release rows remain unchanged.

## WP05-S06-T02 configured retention and restart order

[Configured retention](../../docs/modules/logging/file-retention.md) uses persistent inventory IDs to retain the newest exact listed closed/damaged members, deleting required oldest members under the same lease before further growth. The frozen selected bound reaches the actual writer; no new dependency/API/I/O framework is introduced. Existing startup inventory above a newly selected lower bound fails safely without pruning, preserving the original rollback rule. Maximum readable inventory now rotates by retirement rather than an artificial capacity fault. Sparse IDs, timestamps and fresh producer instances do not reset group identity/order. T03 owns complete deletion-failure/isolation and Human oversized categories; T04 owns broader filesystem cases; S07 owns interrupted updates and damaged-record recovery. Source plans, historical evidence and all 41 Planned release cases remain unchanged.

## WP05-S06-T03 terminal file-policy faults

[File-policy failures](../../docs/modules/logging/file-policy-failures.md) reuse the existing worker terminal path with fixed internal RetentionDeleteFailed and RenderTooLarge categories. Required exact oldest-member deletion faults before further segment growth; healthy console remains usable and ownership releases before child exit. The Human guard is defensive under valid FileMaxBytes/MaxEntryBytes selection and is qualified using actual prepared bytes with a smaller trusted internal segment, without changing public ranges or ordinary input truncation. Only verified native temporary paths receive operational deletion-denial permissions, and those changes restore in finally. No new I/O abstraction, dependency/API or retries are added. Broader filesystem/collision/story qualification remains T04, and interrupted updates/damaged-record recovery remain S07. Source plans, historical evidence and all 41 Planned release cases remain unchanged.

## WP05-S06-T04 physical failure qualification

[Rotation/retention qualification](../../docs/modules/logging/file-rotation-qualification.md) rejects an occupied exact next closed name before retirement, preserving listed evidence for predictable collisions while retaining the non-overwriting move for later races. Existing worker callbacks supply a controlled post-first-physical-write IOException; Linux additionally uses an owned process-only file-size limit for an actual OS-rejected write. These are scoped implementation/qualification choices, not a full-volume, durability or public-lifecycle claim. Failed metadata publication preserves its existing private candidate and closed bytes; reconciliation remains S07. The original four criteria are mapped without changing source plans, historical receipts or the 41 Planned release cases. No shipping I/O framework, dependency, public API or retries are added.

## WP05-S07-T01 inspection before recovery

[Managed boundary inspection](../../docs/modules/logging/jsonl-boundary-inspection.md) checks the owned active JSONL tail only after inventory format/encoding validation, restores position and preserves physical bytes. Actual no-BOM/strict UTF-8/object/LF facts distinguish Empty/Complete/Partial/Invalid. The final-record bound is the absolute supported 16 MiB including LF, rather than a lower newly selected entry cap; earlier records/full event schemas are not revalidated. Empty/Complete may append through the existing worker; Partial/Invalid keep the safe unchanged startup failure until T02 recovery. Human checks, startup size/retention priority, ownership and ordinary caller truncation remain unchanged. This is a scoped implementation choice within T01, without a new public API, dependency, IO framework or full S07 acceptance claim. T02/T03/T04, public bounded lifecycle/provider and release remain required. Source plans, historical receipts and all 41 Planned release rows remain unchanged.

## WP05-S07-T02 partial-segment recovery

[Partial recovery](../../docs/modules/logging/jsonl-partial-recovery.md) reuses the held group lease, persistent segment IDs, complete inventory publication and non-overwriting physical rename. Whole partial segments become distinctly damaged; fresh active uses the next ID. Predictable damaged-name collision, ID exhaustion and full/lower retained bound fail unchanged before growth; startup does not delete pre-existing evidence. A concrete readonly RecoveredPartialSegment output diagnostic freezes successful recovery without inventing current delivery faults or counters. This additive unpublished API fact fulfils the original safe-diagnostics requirement without a new diagnostic/event framework. Current rollback-partial fixtures are renamed rollback-invalid-json and seed malformed terminated JSON because partial startup now recovers. Historical receipts/source plans and all 41 Planned release rows are preserved. T03/T04 interruption/retention/collector qualification and public bounded lifecycle/provider/release remain later work.

T02 review resolves current API-catalog drift by adding the implemented recovery Boolean to the unpublished reference surface and preservation proposal; a compiled shipping/catalog architecture comparison and two negative controls guard it. Historical published baselines/source plans are unchanged. Size priority, visible preflight versus late/inaccessible collision categories, bounded direct-owner reinspection and unexecuted post-rename interruption coverage are documented explicitly.

Category names follow the core component rules.\n/// Supplied rendered messages and exceptions enter core capture. Null or empty EventId.Name becomes\n/// log.message; EventId.Id supplies the integer attribute event_id, including zero and negative values.\n/// Structured state preserves {OriginalFormat} without parsing. Core checks interest before invoking\n/// formatter or copying state; full queues remain interested. Formatter exceptions propagate unchanged;\n/// invalid retained payload increments core diagnostics without output admission. Oversized valid payload\n/// is truncated. Disposed, stopped, filtered and all-faulted calls skip both callbacks after argument guards.\n/// Healthy outputs admit independently. Provider-created scopes are copied at BeginScope; borrowed host\n/// scopes are copied during accepted capture, after Activity and clock snapshots. Combined scope depth is 32.</remarks>\npublic sealed class CoreLoggerProvider : ILoggerProvider, ISupportExternalScope\n{\n    private readonly CoreLogRuntime runtime;\n    private int disposed;\n    private readonly object scopeProviderGate = new();\n    private IExternalScopeProvider? externalScopes;\n\n    /// <summary>Creates a nonowning provider over an existing runtime.</summary>\n    /// <exception cref=\"ArgumentNullException\">Runtime is null.</exception>\n    public CoreLoggerProvider(CoreLogRuntime runtime)\n    {\n        ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(runtime);\n        this.runtime = runtime;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Creates a category logger using the core's validated component name.</summary>\n    /// <exception cref=\"ObjectDisposedException\">This provider has been disposed.</exception>\n    /// <exception cref=\"ArgumentNullException\">Category name is null.</exception>\n    /// <exception cref=\"ArgumentException\">The name does not satisfy the core component rules.</exception>\n    public ILogger CreateLogger(string categoryName)\n    {\n        ObjectDisposedException.ThrowIf(IsDisposed, this);\n        return new CategoryLogger(this, runtime.CreateLogger(categoryName));\n    }\n\n    private bool IsDisposed => Volatile.Read(ref disposed) != 0;\n\n    /// <summary>Idempotently disables this provider; the application retains runtime ownership.</summary>\n    public void Dispose()\n    {\n        lock (scopeProviderGate) { Interlocked.Exchange(ref disposed, 1); Volatile.Write(ref externalScopes, null); }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Borrows the host scope source; accepted events copy its active scopes inline.</summary>\n    /// <remarks>Existing core/provider scopes are the outer layer, host scopes the inner layer, and event attributes replace matching retained keys.\n    /// Host scope lifetime belongs to the host. Provider disposal releases this reference and never disposes host handles.</remarks>\n    /// <exception cref=\"ArgumentNullException\">Scope provider is null.</exception>\n    /// <exception cref=\"ObjectDisposedException\">This provider is disposed.</exception>\n    public void SetScopeProvider(IExternalScopeProvider scopeProvider)\n    {\n        ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(scopeProvider);\n        lock (scopeProviderGate) { ObjectDisposedException.ThrowIf(IsDisposed, this); Volatile.Write(ref externalScopes, scopeProvider); }\n    }\n\n    private static CoreLogLevel Level(LogLevel logLevel)\n    {\n        if (!Enum.IsDefined(logLevel)) { throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(logLevel)); }\n        // The selected v1 Core and Microsoft severity numbers are identical (Trace through None).\n        return (CoreLogLevel)logLevel;\n    }\n\n    private sealed class CategoryLogger(CoreLoggerProvider provider, CoreLogger core) : ILogger\n    {\n        public bool IsEnabled(LogLevel logLevel)\n        {\n            CoreLogLevel level = Level(logLevel);\n            return !provider.IsDisposed && core.IsEnabled(level);\n        }\n\n        public IDisposable? BeginScope<TState>(TState state) where TState : notnull\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(state);\n            ObjectDisposedException.ThrowIf(provider.IsDisposed, provider);\n            if (provider.runtime.IsAdmissionClosed) { throw new InvalidOperationException(\"Cannot begin a scope after runtime admission closes.\"); }\n            return provider.runtime.Scopes.BeginCopiedScope(() => ProviderScopeCapture.Copy(state));\n        }\n\n        public void Log<TState>(LogLevel logLevel, EventId eventId, TState state, Exception? exception,\n            Func<TState, Exception?, string> formatter)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(formatter);\n            CoreLogLevel level = Level(logLevel);\n            if (provider.IsDisposed) { return; }\n            IExternalScopeProvider? scopes = Volatile.Read(ref provider.externalScopes);\n            _ = core.WriteLazy(level, () => new LogEventData\n            {\n                EventName = string.IsNullOrEmpty(eventId.Name) ? \"log.message\" : eventId.Name,\n                Message = formatter(state, exception),\n                Exception = exception,\n                CaptureState = () => ProviderStateCapture.Capture(state, eventId.Id),\n                CaptureScopes = scopes is null ? null : depth => ProviderScopeCapture.Capture(scopes, depth)\n            });\n        }\n    }\n}\n",

--- FILE planning/logging/evidence/wp06-s03-t03/commands/check-guards.ps1 SHA256 093f321b238089c9d09caf3e261575db64be4461cef73aea675aa5839c2b4ce9 ---
param()
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
$repositoryRoot = (Get-Location).Path
$taskRoot = Join-Path $repositoryRoot '.artifacts/csharp/tasks/wp06-s03-t03'
$scriptPath = Join-Path $repositoryRoot 'implementations/csharp/eng/verify-microsoft-logging.ps1'
$tokens = $null; $errors = $null
$ast = [Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile($scriptPath, [ref]$tokens, [ref]$errors)
if ($errors.Count -ne 0) { throw 'Runner parse errors.' }
foreach ($function in $ast.FindAll({ param($node) $node -is [Management.Automation.Language.FunctionDefinitionAst] }, $true)) { Invoke-Expression $function.Extent.Text }
$matrix = Get-Content implementations/csharp/verification/shipping-projects.json -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json
$expectedPaths = @($matrix.shippingProjects[0].moduleSlices[0].sources | ForEach-Object { 'implementations/csharp/' + $_ } | Sort-Object)
$sourceInputs = @($expectedPaths | ForEach-Object { @{path=$_} })
$OutputRoot = Join-Path $taskRoot 'guards'
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $OutputRoot -Force | Out-Null
$results = [Collections.Generic.List[object]]::new()
$native = Join-Path $taskRoot 'mutation-final/mutation/reports/mutation-report.json'
Write-MutationObservation $native
$results.Add(@{id='mutation-positive';passed=$true})
foreach ($case in @('missing', 'empty', 'zero', 'extra')) {
    $report = Get-Content $native -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json
    $adapter = @($report.files.PSObject.Properties | Where-Object { $_.Name.Replace('\', '/') -match '/Logging/MicrosoftLogger/' })
    switch ($case) {
        'missing' { $report.files.PSObject.Properties.Remove($adapter[0].Name) }
        'empty' { $adapter[0].Value.mutants = @() }
        'zero' { foreach ($file in $adapter) { foreach ($mutant in $file.Value.mutants) { $mutant.status = 'Ignored' } } }
        'extra' { $report.files | Add-Member -NotePropertyName ($adapter[0].Name.Replace('CoreLoggerProvider.cs', 'Unexpected.cs')) -NotePropertyValue $adapter[0].Value }
    }
    $path = Join-Path $OutputRoot "$case.json"
    $report | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 100 | Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Encoding utf8
    $failure = $null
    try { Write-MutationObservation $path } catch { $failure = $_.Exception.Message }
    if (-not $failure) { throw "Mutation guard failed to reject $case" }
    $results.Add(@{id="mutation-$case";passed=$true;rejection=$failure})
}
$coverage = Join-Path $taskRoot 'coverage-final/unit'
Write-CoverageObservation $coverage
$results.Add(@{id='coverage-positive';passed=$true})
[xml] $trx = Get-Content (Join-Path $coverage 'report.trx') -Raw
$name = $trx.SelectSingleNode('//*[local-name()="UriAttachment"]/*[local-name()="A"]').GetAttribute('href').Replace('\', '/').Split('/')[-1]
$nativeXml = @(Get-ChildItem $coverage -Recurse -File | Where-Object Name -eq $name)[0]
foreach ($case in @('missing', 'unexecuted')) {
    $directory = Join-Path $OutputRoot "coverage-$case"
    New-Item -ItemType Directory $directory -Force | Out-Null
    Copy-Item (Join-Path $coverage 'report.trx') (Join-Path $directory 'report.trx')
    [xml] $xml = Get-Content $nativeXml.FullName -Raw
    $classes = @($xml.SelectNodes('//class') | Where-Object { $_.GetAttribute('filename') -match 'ProviderScopeCapture.cs$' })
    foreach ($class in $classes) {
        if ($case -eq 'missing') { $null = $class.ParentNode.RemoveChild($class) }
        else { foreach ($line in $class.SelectNodes('./lines/line')) { $line.SetAttribute('hits','0') } }
    }
    $xml.Save((Join-Path $directory $nativeXml.Name))
    $failure = $null
    try { Write-CoverageObservation $directory } catch { $failure = $_.Exception.Message }
    if (-not $failure) { throw "Coverage guard failed to reject $case" }
    $results.Add(@{id="coverage-$case";passed=$true;rejection=$failure})
}
$results | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5 | Set-Content (Join-Path $OutputRoot 'guard-results.json') -Encoding utf8
$results | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 5
